| **Sat 5th** | **Lane 2, the Ropewalk: Seven 6, Ropewalk second four 4.** The second four unlays and walks walls badly. **Yeo and Jude** on the Ropewalk steps: "It's always the same thing… a kitchen in the low town." | 19 |
| **Sun 6th** | Aske: **warm at seven, "a sulk of three nights. Five of six."** Book to Orla. **Orla cutaway:** reads the ferry line; goes to the pump house at six; **meets Hart, badly.** | 19 |
| **Mon 7th (night)** | **Coast night, Gannet Creek**, on the Board's warrant, Company ground: a **kiddle** (seven posts). Kiva second to Pike; **Davy Penhale** (Company catcher) at the purses. A post sheds a rung uncut at the top of the flood (~11 p.m.); Mace's walls laid flat on the water hold part. **High-water slack 11.54 p.m.–12.14 a.m.**; three posts cut from a punt; **"Far edge," once**, at the third. Kiva's fingers on a purse pole go quiet at the slack and come back before the grass moves (sleeve signals). **1.20 a.m.:** Jude with Noa's note (Clerk's order) and Perrin's line. Rhea: "Go." **Rhea's spring advice.** Pru stays. | 20 |
| **Tue 8th** | **Rhea cutaway, low-water slack 6.15:** posts five to seven clean; Pike takes the far edge and the voice; the fourth's "now" a count late; **half a rung into the creek**; closed **6.45** by Bray's crew; six bags and the net's tin, the sixth light. **6 a.m. gate:** Kiva read (stance, one line). **Perrin's test with Anwen: one line.** Noa's answer (not hers; not a stack under any rule; "The lodger did"). **Clerk's ruling** at noon. Carrow: "I'll think about it." **Mara cutaway at dawn:** the **Hoop** rises at 6.14 (Hart's hand quiet 6.12–6.13), stands at **4 ft 6½ in** on the basin staff, 21 minutes, down at 6.35; Peverell's **2604 entry**. | 20, 21 |
| **Wed 9th** | Kiva at the grating with Hart and Jude: quiet 6.13; the Hoop rises; **"It's gone out of the harbour, into that"**; ache 6.33; 4 ft 6½. 7 a.m. Mara shows Kiva the 2604 entry; Kiva's lips move; Hart: "You count when you read." **Adlam** brings the Proprietor's request to see the plate (wired to the Line Sat 5th). | 21 |
| **Thu 10th** | Aske's price. **Sixth crossing**: one cliff, twenty-six, **thirty in all**. "Six." **Notch at six seconds**, all pens; Polglase's book of times (hairs at every reading at every spring since the ninth month). **Orla certifies.** Fourth column: *Certified. Cold. As before.* Hand: **cold to the elbow, an ache in the joint** (noon, four); a hand below at ten. 11.30 **Adlam's bench books** and offer; refused. **The watch's wire.** 7 p.m. **Kellan cutaway** at the Counting House. ~10 p.m. Kellan at the loft: "Don't go alone." | 22 |
| **Fri 11th** | Seventy-seven years after the 2604 night. Hand mid-forearm at seven. **Prediction written and sealed** at the pump house (9.30), received at the Board's press **10.07**, to be opened **noon on the 23rd**. 8 p.m. **the Proprietor**: plate shown, cold; his 2604 account; **the three-door asked for a price; refused.** Fever on the quay (104 by Orla), 108 at the loft, 110 at midnight, **112 at one; broke about three. No dream.** | 23 |
| **Sat 12th** | Aske: no lane. **Lane 3, the Graving: the Tarn first four 5, Seven 4** (Pru catches; "Rowan stands down, on her healer's card"). Kiva watches from the tiers. **Seeding board, 6 p.m.: Seven twelfth of twenty-four.** | 24 |
| **Sun 13th** | Hand cold to the wrist at seven. Perrin wants a second plant. **Trewin** (Tarn captain, still door) asks to learn *nobody*. **Handed crossing rehearsed** in the Company van yard (Finch, Rowan's declared Ember, Trewin): late a count at the second gap. Pike: "You call from the air." Orla takes the book; **tells Kiva the meeting with Hart went badly**; "Six of six… gladder." 6.45 p.m. **Kiva's yes/no log begins** (the outfall-walk rail: yes). Hart: "It went badly on both sides." | 24 |
| **Mon 14th** | **7 a.m. Aske: "Warm." A sulk of four nights. Six of six; certified.** "Thursday's the soonest." Perrin: "Nought." The Bench at nine; Kiva not at the Bench. | 24 |

## 2. Kiva Rowan at exit

- Seventeen; seated; **unclassified**; one door declared at every board **by her captain's voice**
  (declared "stance" at the gate and in lanes 1–2; stood down from lane 3 on her healer's card).
- **Hand to Ember: six of six. Certified** by Orla on Thursday the 10th, in the Chair's ledger:
  *Rowan, K. The second crossing, hand to Ember. Seen six times on sanctioned masts, read by the office's
  reader: four at Meridian, two at the Saltmere gate. Certified, on my line, under her healer's card.
  O. Dane.* **Two crossings now certified**: Ember to still; hand to Ember. One in seven days at most.
- **The fifth** (Thu 3rd, 0.31): white nearly to the elbow at once; 7.15 cold to a hand below the elbow;
  Fri 7 a.m. mid-forearm; Fri 4 p.m. the wrist; **Sun 6th 7 a.m. warm — three nights.**
- **The sixth** (Thu 10th, 0.30): white to the elbow, **an ache in the elbow joint**; noon and 4 p.m. cold to
  the elbow; 10 p.m. a hand below; Fri 7 a.m. mid-forearm; Sun 7 a.m. the wrist; **Mon 14th 7 a.m. warm —
  four nights.** Aske: "the reach is at the elbow now"; he wants the hand warm on more than one morning
  before a seventh; **Thursday the 17th is the soonest** by the card.
- **The notch** at the Saltmere gate mast follows a **single** cliff: **seven seconds** (fifth), **six
  seconds** (sixth), on every pen. In Meridian: eleven, after a second cliff, three times; never after one
  cliff. Noa draws no line. Polglase: "the harbour" / "a coast thing," then "I've no idea."
- **The two fingers:** a bollard, a purse pole, a grating, a banister — *keeps time*. **At slack** (Gannet
  Creek and the basin grating) they say **nothing**, and they report the slack's end before the water
  visibly moves. **Hart's method adopted:** same question, same hour (a quarter to seven), yes or no,
  never *what*; first entry Sun 13th, *yes*.
- **The disk:** cold at every look, including on the Proprietor's table and through the fever.
  Fourth-column lines after the fifth (*Cold. As before.*) and the sixth (*Certified. Cold. As before.*).
- **Fevers:** the ferry (2nd month, with the dream); **Friday the 11th after the Counting House, to 112,
  broke about 3 a.m., no dream** ("Nothing came," said aloud to Aske and Pru, unexplained). Aske's card:
  *Fever, the second this year. Both near a Glory master, within the day.*
- **Near the Proprietor:** her pulse falls to his count (**57**, Orla by the stairhead clock); her Ember
  "wants to march" and her still door "wants to beat"; she holds both shut; one chair further restores it.
  (Contrast Thrale: her doors lay down.)
- **The dream:** not recurred. **Known to nobody new.** The ferry detail that some lights "had gone white"
  is now **Kiva's alone — she has not told Mara**, and kept it from both Mara (over the 2604 entry) and the
  Proprietor (over his lamps). "Unroofed Sky" and "Breach" do not appear.
- **Refused:** the Proprietor's ask for the three-door on a Halden mast for forty drams to Seven's book
  and a seat on his roll. Three doors remain **three clean**; not done.
- **Not done:** no Ember-to-still crossing; no stack; the handed crossing **not live** (one yard rehearsal).
- **Body:** knee still strapped (not mentioned this movement); two fingers as before.

## 3. Team Seven at exit

- **Seeded twelfth of twenty-four**: *Won one, lost two. Points twelve for, sixteen against.*
  - Lane 1 (Wed 2nd, the Ropewalk): **lost 2–7** to the Ropewalk first four.
  - Lane 2 (Sat 5th, the Ropewalk): **won 6–4** against the Ropewalk second four.
  - Lane 3 (Sat 12th, the Graving): **lost 4–5** to the Tarn first four (glass point by the dummy's head).
- **Seeding board (top and Meridian lines on the page):** 1 the Ropewalk first four (won three);
  2 Halden License (won three); 3 the Tarn first four (won three); 6 Old Four; 9 Team Twenty-Eight;
  12 Team Seven; 15 the Ropewalk second four.
- **Corrections learned:** a laid person cannot walk laid; to move they unlay and are nothing for a count
  or two, and their catcher calls it (*run* / *lay*) — **"make it walk"** (Kiva's, in the book in her name).
  Carrow adapts by *walking* where nobody is near and relaying in the path. Pru watches **hands** (a hand
  door); Kiva watches **feet** (a still door).
- **Perrin:** declares for Kiva; asked for and paid for the reading of his stance at Anwen's back; "nobody
  stands together" restated as a rule about the lodger; wants a second plant; Coombe's rope.
- **Anwen:** told Maud Lyle about the lodger and Wenna; taught her to walk a wall from its far end; admitted
  Lyle's laid wall is better in a lane; did not tell Perrin.
- **Jude:** out of the sling for lanes only (Aske); stood well in lanes 1–3; met **Yeo** ("not yet");
  saw the Hoop; "the slack's for… twenty minutes when you can see what's keeping time."
- **Pru:** hauled drawstrings on the coast night; **stayed** when Kiva was called; caught lane 3; "not
  grateful"; watches hands.
- **Trewin** (Tarn) rehearsed as plant once, late a count. Not a member of Seven.

## 4. The book (Jude keeps it)

| Date | Entry | Held |
|---|---|---:|
| Carried, the 30th of the second month | | 6.23 |
| Wed 2nd | Lane 1 (Pryce 0.21; Cade 0.12; Talley 0.10) | −0.43 → 5.80 |
| Thu 3rd | Hand to Ember, the fifth | −0.31 → 5.49 |
| Sat 5th | Lane 2 (Pryce 0.18; Cade 0.10; Talley 0.12) | −0.40 → 5.09 |
| Tue 8th | The captain's reading at the gate (Pryce 0.06; Cade 0.03) | −0.09 → 5.00 |
| Thu 10th | Hand to Ember, the sixth | −0.30 → **4.70** |
| Thu 10th | Offered: 12 drams for the sealed 2.0 plain (Adlam); **refused** | 0.00 |
| Sat 12th | Lane 3 (Pryce 0.19; Cade 0.14; Talley 0.09; Finch not lit) | −0.42 → **4.28** |
| Sun 13th | The handed crossing, a yard, borrowed still door (Finch 0.12; Trewin his own) | −0.12 → **4.16** |

Check: 6.23 − 2.07 = **4.16.** On the page: "four and seventy hundredths" (Kiva, Fri 11th; Jude on the
10th: "five, near enough"); "four and twenty-eight hundredths" (Jude, Sun 13th); "Held, four and sixteen"
(Jude's entry). **The per-line breakdown of lanes and the gate reading is the author's reconciliation;
only the totals named are on the page.** Spoken for: a seventh crossing when the card allows (~0.30), not
yet entered. **Sealed, medical, W, plain: 2.0, unopened**, bound *For a hand. Not for a bank, a floor, a
crossing or a sale.* Combed at the gate Tue 1st: W full, I silent. Not on the book: Kiva's thimble (her
stance; the Ember carry on the 13th); Pru's pay; the coast night (a second draws no share).

**Graywater's coast slate (Tue 8th):** *Closed: a quarter to seven, by the Company, on the Board's
warrant. Lives: none. Works: the marsh grass of Gannet Creek, frosted for a quarter mile inland. A sheep.
Tinned: six bags, and the net's tin. The sixth bag light.* Remarks three (the shed rung, the creek's; the
second's far edge and slack; the officer's going short: "She wrote the card"). Penhale weighs at the
Company yard; no figure on the page. Earlier: about twenty drams lost to the creek on the flood (Penhale's
guess); half the fourth post's rung lost at dawn ("twenty drams, perhaps," Rhea).

## 5. Saltmere, as on the page

- **The Mole** (a mile of curved sea wall); **the sea gate** (black iron on chains in a stone arch, ten feet
  above the water; down only in gales; not since the winter before last); **the low town** below high water,
  kept dry by the pumps; drains with iron gratings that rattle on the count; **the high town** on a ridge.
- **The harbour pump house** (*Built 2398. Re-celled 2611.*): thirty-six cells on two galleries, eighteen a
  side; **six pumps** on one stroke; keeper's desk and **press** of books back to 2398; **outfall gallery**,
  iron landing, ladder, **grating** over a culvert; **the basin's tide staff** (0 at the mud to 24 ft).
- **The basin's slack lags the sea gate's table by three-and-twenty minutes** (Mara found it Wed 2nd night).
- **The Regiment:** the bank's name on the bills. **Fifty-seven to the minute** (Adlam, timed Sat 5th).
- **The Ropewalk:** a quarter-mile tarred shed on the ridge; pen at the near end twelve paces wide; the
  **Commodore** (canvas dummy, tarred hat, painted moustache). Up **Tar Lane**.
- **The Graving:** a dry dock three hundred paces long, as deep as a church, tiers roofed with iron and
  glass, a floating gate at the seaward end; floor eighty feet down. The Board's **gate** is in the old
  **timber pond** beside it, half boarded; the **gate mast** (forty feet, eight pens).
- **The Long Quay**, **Quay Street**, **Quiller's** chandlery (Meridian's loft), the **Company's van yard**,
  the **Counting House** (Halden's house on the Long Quay; white unflickering lamps; the long room).
- **The Longshore:** flats east; **Gannet Creek**; an old stone causeway ("the hard").
- **Coast craft (as on the page):** cut only at the **slack**; ward-walls laid **flat on water** as a boom;
  **purses** — sheets on iron rings with drawstrings, set at low water, hauled from the bank; "a shut purse
  is a sealed bag" on this coast; Penhale's count "one fathom, two fathom."
- **A kiddle:** seven grey posts across a creek mouth, skins between them like nets; "works" the flood and
  the ebb (leans with the water; water through the skin comes out frosted); **stands upright at slack**; can
  **shed a rung uncut** at the top of a surge; a cut post's skin **folds from its far edge** toward the
  nearest line. Tear in the bed under the middle post, closed at low water by a licensed crew.

## 6. The Hoop and the prediction

- **The Hoop** (low-town children's name; Peverell's "the ring at the outfall"): a grey ring, frost on its lip,
  "as wide as a cart is long," rising from the mud ten paces out from the grating **at the start of a spring
  low-water slack** and lying down at its end. Inside it, harbour water **held level with its lip**, above the
  basin, **trembling four and four at the bank's count**. While it stands, **Hart's hand on the grating is
  quiet** (nothing in the basin keeps time) though the pumps run. It lies down and the held water spills in a
  white sheet. First noted the **26th of the ninth month, a hand high**; **4 ft 1 in** at the springs a
  fortnight before the 8th; **4 ft 6½ in** on the 8th and the 9th. Stood 6.14–6.35 (8th), 6.13–6.33 (9th).
- **Readings voiced (not established):** Hart: "as if the count goes out of the harbour at the slack and into
  that." Mara (the 8th) and Kiva (the 9th) said the same. Jude: "twenty minutes when you can see what's
  keeping time." Mara: "the harbour was still at the slack. All of it, except one thing."
- **Seen by:** Hart, Peverell, Yeo, Rusk, Mara (8th); Hart, Kiva, Jude, Mara, Peverell (9th); the low town's
  children at the springs.
- **The prediction**, written by Mara, sealed by Peverell with the pump house seal, received at the Board's
  press at 10.07 on Fri 11th, **to be opened at noon on the 23rd**:
  > *Saltmere harbour pump house. The 11th of the third month, 2681, at half past nine in the morning. At the
  > low water of the dawn on Wednesday the 23rd of this month, a spring tide, the slack in the basin will begin
  > at nine minutes past six by the pump house clock, and not before seven past nor after twelve past. It will
  > last not less than eighteen minutes nor more than twenty-two. When it begins, the ring at the outfall will
  > rise from the mud. It will stand at four feet eleven inches on the basin's staff, and not less than four
  > feet ten. It will lie down when the slack ends. While it stands, the water inside it will keep the bank's
  > count, four and four, at fifty-seven to the minute or faster. Nothing else in the basin will keep any count,
  > by the hand on the grating, though the pumps run. We do not know why. We write it so that it can be found
  > wrong. J. Hart, fitter. M. Rowan, Grade Two.* Witnessed, Peverell, keeper, *who does not know why either*;
  > Yeo, officer. *Watched written: Rusk, Examiner* (Rusk would not sign for the Works).
- The Harbour Committee has written to the Clerk three times asking whether the Works means to stop the
  Regiment during the Measure. The Clerk will say there is a paper in the press, not what it says.

## 7. The 2604 Saltmere keeper's entry

Found by **Peverell** (four whole-house remarks in three hundred years; this one by searching, not by date).
Hand leaning the other way from Ferren's, long *s*. On the page:

> *The 11th of the third month. Spring tide. At the low water in the evening a ring stood up in the basin at the
> outfall, a hand high, and the water in it would not go down with the rest. Lay down at the flood. Not in the
> fault column; nothing was at fault. / Took a fever after, the keeper. Two nights. / Dreamed there was no roof
> on the house nor on anything, and nothing over. The nothing was not empty and it was not dark. Something was
> attending. I write it here because my father told me to write what the needles did, and they did it too. /
> The refinery's lamps on the Long Quay went white about three in the morning and stayed lit. The low town's
> went out. They came on again of themselves at the half hour. Not grid.*

Mara asked Peverell to keep the page under lock and let nobody copy it until she has written to Lumb.

## 8. The Proprietor (Ambrose Halden) as on the page

- Looks about fifty-five; dark hair grey at the temples; lean; plain dark coat. **States he is a hundred and
  three**; was **twenty-six** and **a bench clerk in the Counting House** in 2604.
- **His limit:** his **left hand lies on a glass case over the foundation cell** (the first stepped, 2391,
  "by a man whose name nobody remembers"). **He has not lifted it for more than a minute since 2611**, when
  the holder before him died with his hand on the glass. He eats with his right; a servant cuts his meat;
  he sleeps with the case by the bed. He cannot rise to greet. "I don't know what would happen if I let go…
  I'm not brave enough."
- **Effect:** people near him "fall in with it" — pulse slows to his count (Kellan felt it; Kiva to 57);
  "I've had clerks faint"; "nobody's ever fainted at the far end."
- **On the night of 2604 (his words):** he had **a fever** from the evening; at about three he saw every lamp
  on the Long Quay (Halden's, stepped) go white and stay lit and every lamp in the low town go out for half an
  hour. He did **not** speak of a dream. His reading: "the things that kept time together went white… the
  things that kept their own time… went out." He would step the country if allowed. "I may be wrong about
  both." He hopes Mara does it well and is right; "I'd rather be wrong than have the low town flood."
- **The plate:** asked properly; **the watch voted four hands, three fists**: *shown in her own hand, at a
  table, to whom she chooses, if she chooses; no hand but hers; not weighed, tested, drawn or copied.* Kiva
  showed it on his table, hand on the cord. Cold. "I thought it might be warm, near me. That's vanity."
- **The ask:** the three-door on his mast at the refinery beside a stepped bank of twelve, with his reader and
  Noa, Aske, Orla, every condition; price **forty drams to Seven's book** and **a seat on his roll at a Torch's
  wage** under any healer's card. **Refused** (reasons on the page: it lives in one hand; cost and the unknown
  fourth return; a price tonight is a higher price next week; offered instead a certified crossing on his mast
  any week; "the country can ask… in the Chair's hand"). He laughed; "I find I'm glad you said no."
  Orla and Perrin silent by Kiva's request; Orla: "She said no for herself"; Perrin: "Under my name as well."

## 9. Other people at exit

| Person | State |
|---|---|
| **Mara Rowan** | At Saltmere since Wed 2nd (Works' transit, Rusk). Found the 23-minute lag. Has **not read the bank** and **has not touched a cell**; reading hand kept in her pocket "until she had decided"; has "found the next question." Sealed the prediction with Hart. Shown the 2604 entry to Kiva only. Will write to Lumb. Told Kiva she needn't tell the Proprietor anything. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Met. Tall, stooped, about forty; fitter's blue jacket; watch on the inside of the left wrist (turns it before answering); black hair grey over the ears; brown deep-set eyes; coast voice with Meridian under it. **Right hand** turned out at the Regional's Bench (twenty-one years ago): whole in shape, waxy grey-white, fingers curled, feels nothing, holds nothing, **aches when something near keeps time**; quiet at slack. Writes left-handed. **Book of slacks** (*Date. Slack, by the watch. Hand. Staff.*). Teaches: ask the same question at the same hour, yes or no. Has his own shut doors; does not ask Kiva hers. Saw her lips count. Met Orla once, badly; told Kiva "badly on both sides." |
| **Orla Dane** | Came by the Circuit's transit; on a fish crate at the mast. **Certified the second crossing.** Read the ferry line and did not ask. **Met Hart Sunday 6th, badly** (told him where he *should* be; began "She's—" and stopped; he: "There."). Told Kiva herself on the 13th. At the Counting House, silent by request. |
| **Peverell** (keeper, she) | Small, upright, spectacles on a cord, apron; crabbed hand; nine years keeper; "my cells"; "I'll not be the keeper who stopped the pumps. If they're stopped, I'll be the keeper who wrote down that somebody else did." Witnessed the prediction. |
| **Yeo** (Company officer, she) | Tall, grey-haired, brisk; notebook; watched Jude; "Keeping the sea out… a kitchen in the low town." Witnessed the prediction. No draft made. |
| **Examiner Rusk** | At Saltmere; watched the Hoop; wrote in a black book; would not sign the prediction for the Works ("Watched written"). |
| **Mr. Adlam** | Bench books: a stepped row **fell in at 48 a minute** (2402, 2431); 49 about 2550; **50 in 2604**; 52 by 2650; 55 in the ninth month of 2680; **57** last month. The harbour bank also **57**. Offered **12 drams for Seven's 2.0 plain** (for his comb's seventh reed); took no; "I'll tell them you said no, and why." Carried the Proprietor's request; wired the Line on Sat 5th. |
| **Noa Bexley** | Read every crossing and the Ropewalk's lay; entered the lay as "a stack… an incident" under the rule; answered the ch. 13 question (§10). Copied Adlam's figures into the foot of his **third** column; considered a fourth and did not draw it. |
| **W. Polglase** | Saltmere's reader, forty years on the gate mast; steel spectacles; "Laid." Kept a book of times; on the 10th found hairs at every reading at every spring since the ninth month; "I've no idea." |
| **The Clerk of the Measure** | Small, grey, gold chain; "shut in a great many rooms"; ruled on the lay (Tue 8th); keeps the Board's press (three locks). Unnamed. |
| **Juniper Carrow** | Ropewalk captain; edge and stance laid; adapted (walked, relaid in the path); "I'll think about it. I'll not stop." |
| **Maud Lyle** | Ropewalk Hold; ward and stance laid; caught running in lane 1; told by Anwen of the lodger and Wenna; walked a wall on one door ("It's so loud"). |
| **Tolver, Treen** | Ropewalk carry (long-armed, cheerful) and catcher (small, dark, calls *run/lay*, "likes to be noticed"). |
| **Wenna** | A Ropewalk girl who lost a laid door on the Longshore in the eleventh month; teaches the little ones. Named only. |
| **Trewin** | Tarn first-four captain; still door; "face like a gatepost"; asked to learn *nobody*; rehearsed once, late. |
| **The Tarn reserve** | Plaited girl, ~15; her grandfather's chandlery at Tarnfoot; soot-cut jar. Unnamed. |
| **Davy Penhale** | Saltmere Company catcher, about thirty, red-bearded, talkative; "one fathom, two fathom"; ordinary colleague. Nothing telegraphed. |
| **Bray** | Company closing officer at Gannet Creek; "a voice like a foghorn." |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Came south on the Board's warrant. **Went short** on the 7th–8th by her own card (half a rung, a remark in her name). **Spring's advice given** ("When you're called, go. Don't look back at the creek."). **One night left** on the card; it ends on the 30th. Privately aware how much she leans on her second. |
| **Pike** | Hands out of dressings Fri 4th; close halfway. Voice on the coast night; took far edge and voice at dawn; "now" a count late by choice of eyes. Was Kiva's second in the yard on the 13th ("You call from the air"). |
| **Mace's line** | Walls laid flat on water learned on the coast ("in two hours"). Tibb, Stroud named. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Halden License (won its seeding lanes; seeded second). Dined at the Counting House Thu 10th; gave the Proprietor only the record; refused her count; told him she'd say no; **told Kiva** the same night. Felt his pulse fall in. |
| **Pettit** | "He's the best man I ever sat at a table with… I'd not want him to ask me for anything I couldn't give." |
| **Selka Dray** | One stick at arrival; on both feet without it by the 10th and the 14th. "That's fairer." "I've not changed my mind about you." |
| **Healer Aske** | Says the price before each crossing; keeps the card; "soonest." |

## 10. The ch. 13 open thread — retired

At Perrin's request on Tue 8th, with two readers and the Clerk present, Anwen's ward with Perrin's stance at
her back read **one line on the sanctioned gate mast**. Noa: not Rowan's (she was not in it; no incident of hers
on the Marshal's floor or anywhere); **not a stack under any rule** (a stack is one person's two doors); "Whose:
both. The tape does not know the difference. The lodger did." Polglase: Saltmere does not teach two-person lays;
fishermen holding a net do it. The Clerk entered it **"in no circuit's column."** Perrin: "nobody stands together"
is a rule about the lodger.

## 11. The Clerk's ruling (Tue 8th, as posted)

Readings under the rule stand as readings; whether a reading is an incident is for the candidate's own circuit;
a lay is entered in the Saltmere column and is not an incident for host candidates; the Meridian Chair's
conditions bind Meridian candidates; every reading under the rule is posted for the draft boards and the Board.

## 12. Knowledge boundaries, changed

| Fact | Now known to |
|---|---|
| That Kiva dreams | **Nobody new.** |
| The ferry dream's "some had gone white" | **Kiva only** (not Mara). |
| The Saltmere 2604 entry (fever, no roof, "something was attending", the ring, lamps white / out) | Peverell (found it), **Mara**, **Kiva**. Hart, Yeo and Rusk were in the room on the 8th; whether they read the page is not stated. Hart has read his keeper's books generally (not stated for this page). |
| That the Proprietor had a fever on the 2604 night and saw the lamps | Kiva, Perrin, Orla (his words). He did not mention a dream. |
| That Kiva has fevers near Glory masters | Aske (card), Pru, Orla, Perrin; Kellan saw the night. Content: none. |
| The Proprietor's ask and Kiva's refusal | Kiva, Perrin, Orla; Kellan and Pettit expected it. |
| Orla and Hart met, badly | Orla, Hart, Kiva (told by both). |
| The Hoop's behaviour; the prediction's text | Hart, Mara, Peverell, Yeo, Rusk, Kiva, Jude; the Clerk holds it sealed. |
| The Step quickening (48 → 57) | Adlam, Seven, Noa (copied); the Proprietor (Adlam reports to him). |
| The notch after a single cliff, 7 then 6 s | Noa, Polglase, Seven, Orla, Aske. |
| The lodger and Wenna, from Coombe | Lyle, Carrow (via Lyle). |
| What Hart dreamed, if anything | **Nobody. Kiva did not ask.** |

## 13. New names, places and terms (pending owner review)

Checked by search against every manuscript, planning and provenance file in this repository and Book One's
manuscript before use (`universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md` present; no collision found for these): **Quiller** (chandler),
**Polglase** (reader, initial W.), **Tolver**, **Treen**, **Wenna**, **Davy Penhale**, **Bray**, **Trewin**,
**Tarnfoot**, **the Tarn** (circuit), **Tar Lane**, **the Long Quay**, **Quay Street**, **the Counting House**,
**the Mole**, **the timber pond**, **Gannet Creek**, **the Commodore** (dummy), **the Regiment** (the bank),
**the Hoop**, **a kiddle**, **gappers** (Saltmere slang for Meridian), **purse** (coast catch). Rejected for
collision during the run: Hendra (Hendry Moss), Pell (Pell Garrow), Curb (common noun), Jory, Kit, Abel, Rugg, Admiral (Mr. Garland's bird, Book One), Bet (near Bettony), Alderman (used elsewhere in the series).
Unnamed: the Clerk; the Tarn reserve; the Ropewalk second four's captain and catcher; the Line's agent; the gate
clerk; Halden's livery men; the Company boatman; the bosun.

## 14. Departures from the packet and items for attention

1. **Length 54,482 words** (`wc -w`), inside 50,000–56,000.
2. **Viewpoint: Kiva 82.5%** (44,930); cutaways **9,552** — Anwen 1,398; Orla 1,872; Rhea 2,022; Mara 2,429;
   Kellan 1,831. **52 words over the 9,500 ceiling.** Recorded, not trimmed.
3. **Sections: 79**, about 690 words a section (formula target ~950; ~8.7 breaks per 10k words). Above the
   target frequency.
4. **Crossings placed Thursdays (3rd, 10th),** not Sundays: Aske ruled "Thursday at the soonest" after the coast
   boat; Thursday the 10th keeps a crossing available from Thursday the 17th (the Lane), and the 23rd (the
   Slack) is reachable either way. The sixth is Orla's certification. Aske says the price before each.
5. **Lane 3 caught by Pru** because of the Friday fever. This is a seeding lane, not a national board; the map's
   "Pru catches a national board" (Five) is still open.
6. **The Proprietor states a fever on the 2604 night** (no dream). This plants his Movement Five testimony and
   does not spend it.
7. **A second fever without a dream** after Glory contact; Aske's card links the two fevers to Glory masters.
8. **The Hoop** is the author's (ring; held water at the bank's count; stands only at slack; the basin otherwise
   quiet by the hand). The **2604 Saltmere entry records a ring a hand high** on the same night. The prediction
   says what will happen; it does not say what the Hoop is.
9. **The notch** now follows a single cliff on the coast (seven, then six seconds). Publicly "the harbour" /
   "a coast thing."
10. **The Step's count quickening (48 → 57)** and the harbour bank at 57 are on the page (bible §7.4).
11. **The Order's binding honoured**: Adlam's fair offer refused on the binding, aloud.
12. **Soot-cut stock** surfaced once, small and human (the Tarn reserve's grandfather's jar; the clerk: "It's not
    a crime that I know of. It's a pity.").
13. **Rhea went short** (half a rung; her remark), honouring the card; one night remains.
14. **The handed crossing rehearsed once with a borrowed link** (Trewin), on a yard; not live.
15. **Not done:** the Bench, Carry, Hunt, Lane brackets, Graving boards; the Slack; any three-door; any stack; any
    Saltmere Company death; the point of light; the hull; any explanation of the Step, the notch, the Hoop, the
    disk or the dream. No villain.
16. **Formula measures** (sentence distribution, paragraph proxy, readability, progression and combat density,
    development cadence) were written toward and **have not been measured** in the author run.

## 15. Dates ahead

The Bench, Monday the 14th, nine o'clock (Kiva not at the Bench). The Carry, Tuesday the 15th (Perrin's). The
Hunt on the Longshore. **The soonest seventh crossing: Thursday the 17th**, hand warm on more than one morning,
price said first. The Lane, the 17th–19th. **The prediction opens at noon on Wednesday the 23rd** (the dawn
slack). Rhea's last night on the card before the 30th.
